# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/leds/ti,tlc59116.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `LEDs connected to tlc59116 or tlc59108`. In this tree it is a LED class Devicetree binding schema; it constrains how DTS/DTB nodes describe the hardware and how Linux drivers and framework code discover resources through OF matching, phandles, common binding references, and validated child-node topology.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This file is declarative schema rather than executable code. Its public ABI surface is `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/leds/ti,tlc59116.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples` consumed by dt-schema. Compatible strings are `ti,tlc59108`, `ti,tlc59116`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`; conditional or nested required properties visible in composed schemas are none. Important properties include `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `compatible`, `reg`. Property roles include `#address-cells` (child address encoding), `#size-cells` (child size encoding), `compatible` (driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier), `reg` (MMIO resource, bus address, or child register address). Direct and nested schema references are `common.yaml#`. Local schema definitions are none. The top-level closure rule is `additionalProperties: false`. Maintainers: `Andrew Lunn <andrew@lunn.ch>`.

**Control flow:** Runtime behavior is supplied by Devicetree tooling and kernel subsystems, not by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves references such as `common.yaml#`, applies `properties`, `required`, enum/const/items limits, pattern properties, local definitions, and composition keywords, then validates the inline examples. At boot, a compiled DTB node matching one of the compatible strings lets the OF core bind the relevant driver; framework code then reads resources such as LED channel indexes, color/function naming, trigger source links, and regulator/GPIO enable wiring. Schema composition points in this file are `allOf`, `if`, `then`. Nested or patterned child-node surfaces include `^led@[0-9a-f]$`; patternProperties are `^led@[0-9a-f]$`.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: board DTS files and shipped DTBs encode the compatible strings, resource ordering, cell counts, phandle topology, supplies, graph links, and child-node names validated here. Tightening required fields, changing enum ordering, altering `#*-cells`, or changing the closure policy can break existing board descriptions even though the file itself is static documentation plus validation rules.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Integrated with Linux LED class, LED trigger, and I2C/platform LED driver probing. The schema depends on dt-schema core handling plus referenced schemas `common.yaml#`. Driver integration is through `compatible`; subsystem integration is through standard properties, provider/client cell properties, OF graph `ports` and `endpoint` nodes where present, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, power domains, GPIOs, IOMMU mappings, DMA/mailbox phandles, and any SoC-specific properties declared in this file.

**Risks:** Main risks are schema ABI drift, missing or overly broad compatible coverage, incorrect required resources, wrong `#*-cells` or graph lane counts, examples that do not exercise conditional branches, and closure settings that either reject valid legacy DTS extensions or allow misspelled properties. For media, mailbox, LED, and CPU/platform bindings, parent/child address-cell mismatches, endpoint graph mistakes, trigger/provider phandle errors, and firmware-facing mailbox channel semantics are especially easy to miss if only the schema example is tested.

**Test signals:** Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/leds/ti,tlc59116.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` against DTS users of every compatible family listed above. Verify inline examples compile, reference schemas resolve, required-property failures are reported, compatible-specific conditional branches are exercised, and the matched Linux driver still probes with the resources described here. This file contains 1 top-level `examples` block(s), which are part of the dt-schema validation signal.

In this tree it is a media or V4L2 Devicetree binding schema; it constrains how DTS/DTB nodes describe the hardware and how Linux drivers and framework code discover resources through OF matching, phandles, common binding references, and validated child-node topology.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This file is declarative schema rather than executable code. Its public ABI surface is `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/media/cec/st,stih-cec.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples` consumed by dt-schema. Compatible strings are `st,stih-cec`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `clocks`, `hdmi-phandle`, `interrupts`, `resets`, `reg`; conditional or nested required properties visible in composed schemas are none. Important properties include `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `interrupt-names`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `resets`. Property roles include `clock-names` (semantic labels for clock inputs), `clocks` (clock provider phandles), `compatible` (driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier), `interrupt-names` (semantic labels for interrupt entries), `interrupts` (interrupt line routing), `reg` (MMIO resource, bus address, or child register address), `resets` (reset-controller phandles). Direct and nested schema references are `cec-common.yaml#`. Local schema definitions are none. The top-level closure rule is `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Maintainers: `Alain Volmat <alain.volmat@foss.st.com>`.

**Control flow:** Runtime behavior is supplied by Devicetree tooling and kernel subsystems, not by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves references such as `cec-common.yaml#`, applies `properties`, `required`, enum/const/items limits, pattern properties, local definitions, and composition keywords, then validates the inline examples. At boot, a compiled DTB node matching one of the compatible strings lets the OF core bind the relevant driver; framework code then reads resources such as OF graph ports/endpoints, media bus lane descriptions, clocks/resets/regulators, and video/IR/CEC data paths. Schema composition points in this file are `allOf`. Nested or patterned child-node surfaces include none; patternProperties are none.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: board DTS files and shipped DTBs encode the compatible strings, resource ordering, cell counts, phandle topology, supplies, graph links, and child-node names validated here. Tightening required fields, changing enum ordering, altering `#*-cells`, or changing the closure policy can break existing board descriptions even though the file itself is static documentation plus validation rules.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Integrated with Linux media controller/V4L2, remote-control, CEC, camera sensor, CSI, and codec drivers. The schema depends on dt-schema core handling plus referenced schemas `cec-common.yaml#`. Driver integration is through `compatible`; subsystem integration is through standard properties, provider/client cell properties, OF graph `ports` and `endpoint` nodes where present, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, power domains, GPIOs, IOMMU mappings, DMA/mailbox phandles, and any SoC-specific properties declared in this file.

**Risks:** Main risks are schema ABI drift, missing or overly broad compatible coverage, incorrect required resources, wrong `#*-cells` or graph lane counts, examples that do not exercise conditional branches, and closure settings that either reject valid legacy DTS extensions or allow misspelled properties. For media, mailbox, LED, and CPU/platform bindings, parent/child address-cell mismatches, endpoint graph mistakes, trigger/provider phandle errors, and firmware-facing mailbox channel semantics are especially easy to miss if only the schema example is tested.

**Test signals:** Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/cec/st,stih-cec.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` against DTS users of every compatible family listed above. Verify inline examples compile, reference schemas resolve, required-property failures are reported, compatible-specific conditional branches are exercised, and the matched Linux driver still probes with the resources described here. This file contains 1 top-level `examples` block(s), which are part of the dt-schema validation signal.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/isil,isl79987.yaml

**Purpose:** Defines the Devicetree JSON-schema contract for `Intersil ISL79987 Analog to MIPI CSI-2 decoder`. In this tree it is a media or V4L2 Devicetree binding schema; it constrains how DTS/DTB nodes describe the hardware and how Linux drivers and framework code discover resources through OF matching, phandles, common binding references, and validated child-node topology. The source description says: The Intersil ISL79987 is an analog to MIPI CSI-2 decoder which is capable of receiving up to four analog stream and multiplexing them into up to four MIPI CSI-2 virtual channels, using one MIPI clock lane and 1/2 data lanes.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** This file is declarative schema rather than executable code. Its public ABI surface is `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/media/i2c/isil,isl79987.yaml#`, `$schema` `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, schema composition keywords, and inline `examples` consumed by dt-schema. Compatible strings are `isil,isl79987`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `ports`; conditional or nested required properties visible in composed schemas are `data-lanes`, `port@0`. Important properties include `compatible`, `ports`, `powerdown-gpios`, `reg`, `reset-gpios`. Property roles include `compatible` (driver match key and stable hardware ABI identifier), `ports` (OF graph endpoint topology), `reg` (MMIO resource, bus address, or child register address), `reset-gpios` (GPIO reset control). Direct and nested schema references are `/schemas/graph.yaml#/$defs/port-base`, `/schemas/graph.yaml#/properties/port`, `/schemas/graph.yaml#/properties/ports`, `/schemas/media/video-interfaces.yaml#`. Local schema definitions are none. The top-level closure rule is `additionalProperties: false`. Maintainers: `Michael Tretter <m.tretter@pengutronix.de>`, `Marek Vasut <marex@denx.de>`.

**Control flow:** Runtime behavior is supplied by Devicetree tooling and kernel subsystems, not by this YAML file. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves references such as `/schemas/graph.yaml#/$defs/port-base`, `/schemas/graph.yaml#/properties/port`, `/schemas/graph.yaml#/properties/ports`, `/schemas/media/video-interfaces.yaml#`, applies `properties`, `required`, enum/const/items limits, pattern properties, local definitions, and composition keywords, then validates the inline examples. At boot, a compiled DTB node matching one of the compatible strings lets the OF core bind the relevant driver; framework code then reads resources such as OF graph ports/endpoints, media bus lane descriptions, clocks/resets/regulators, and video/IR/CEC data paths. Schema composition points in this file are none. Nested or patterned child-node surfaces include `^port@[1-4]$`, `ports`; patternProperties are `^port@[1-4]$`.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema owns no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: board DTS files and shipped DTBs encode the compatible strings, resource ordering, cell counts, phandle topology, supplies, graph links, and child-node names validated here. Tightening required fields, changing enum ordering, altering `#*-cells`, or changing the closure policy can break existing board descriptions even though the file itself is static documentation plus validation rules.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Integrated with Linux media controller/V4L2, remote-control, CEC, camera sensor, CSI, and codec drivers. The schema depends on dt-schema core handling plus referenced schemas `/schemas/graph.yaml#/$defs/port-base`, `/schemas/graph.yaml#/properties/port`, `/schemas/graph.yaml#/properties/ports`, `/schemas/media/video-interfaces.yaml#`. Driver integration is through `compatible`; subsystem integration is through standard properties, provider/client cell properties, OF graph `ports` and `endpoint` nodes where present, interrupts, clocks, resets, regulators, power domains, GPIOs, IOMMU mappings, DMA/mailbox phandles, and any SoC-specific properties declared in this file.

**Risks:** Main risks are schema ABI drift, missing or overly broad compatible coverage, incorrect required resources, wrong `#*-cells` or graph lane counts, examples that do not exercise conditional branches, and closure settings that either reject valid legacy DTS extensions or allow misspelled properties. For media, mailbox, LED, and CPU/platform bindings, parent/child address-cell mismatches, endpoint graph mistakes, trigger/provider phandle errors, and firmware-facing mailbox channel semantics are especially easy to miss if only the schema example is tested.
